done

The readability series now counts return-type lies, and the how-to guide has the day-1 lesson about them.

**The series.** `tools/readability_progress.py` has two new columns, `return_lie_fns` and `return_lie_sites`. They are read from the census's return-lie counts, and older rows show them blank. Two rows were added:
- **Baseline:** 273 functions and 100,266 sites, a partial row at T3's commit 233ab84eb9. Only the return-lie cells are filled.
- **After T4:** 19 functions and 9,302 sites, all 19 ledgered in `config/lying_exceptions.tsv`. This is a real snapshot at HEAD 01d5b130ec.

**The README graph.** It is drawn by `tools/timeline.py`, which was not in the task's file list. I had it edited anyway, because without that the graph cannot show the drop. It now draws a green line for return lies in the lower panel. A test render to `/home/musashi/bfm-decomp/.run/P41/T13/timeline.svg` shows the line. The committed graph is left for T11 to regenerate. Both rows are dated 2026-11-07, so the line is drawn as a short step with dots.

**The guide.** I wrote the lesson into `docs/how-to-ai-decomp/03-bootstrap-order.md` (a new Phase 0 item and checklist line 1) and into `docs/how-to-ai-decomp/10-integration-and-propagation.md` (an honesty-rules bullet). It covers:
- declare each function once in a shared header every file includes, so gcc rejects definition/declaration mismatches;
- compare return types as well as arity;
- a void-to-int declaration can still move bytes under gcc-2.7.2 (func_80139BE0, 3.41 T4.c1), so load-bearing ones keep a cast at the call and an exception row.

**Checks.** `doc_links --strict` reports 0 pending and 0 broken, and `make tools-health` ended OK in the coder's run.

**Commits.** 96e4726f58 is the coder's (T13.c1). 207539053b holds the guide edits, the log and the summary.

**For T10.** The new snapshot row's `uncovered` cell is blank, because `.run/P39/census/residue_census.json` is older than its `type_census.json` and the staleness check refused it. Before taking the close snapshot, T10 should run `PY tools/type_census.py --check-casts --residue` against the census the series reads, so the close row gets that number.

Summary: `/home/musashi/bfm-decomp/phase-ends/current/tasks/T13.md`
Log: `/home/musashi/bfm-decomp/phase-ends/current/logs/T13.md`

done

T12 is committed at 5b8203738b, and nothing the project needs is left only in .run/P41/. Almost everything there was scratch whose content is already in the repo: the cookbook entries C0547 to C0554, config/ghidra/types/, config/lying_exceptions.tsv and the task summaries. Two things were not, and both are now in place:

- **ghidra_types.py scratch path:** tools/ghidra_types.py wrote its gcc probe into .run/P41/T7/gcc_compare, so every later `--gcc-compare` run would have recreated a P41 folder. It now writes to build/ghidra_types/gcc_compare, which `make clean` wipes. The old path is kept in a comment.
- **MCP client promoted:** T8's SSE MCP client is now tools/ghidra_mcp_call.py (`list` and `call <tool> <json>`). It is the only way a subagent without MCP tools can query the live Ghidra server. It has a tool_dictionary row, and cookbook C0554 points to it instead of telling readers to rewrite it. I have not run it against a live server: the MCP is stopped (R72), so no :8080 call was made.

`make kit-corpus` ran. Besides the new tool, it also caught up the kit's copies of story.md, retrospective.md and plan_edit.py, which were behind already-committed sources. `make tools-health` passed (`tools-health: OK`), but only on the second try: the first run hit the 280 s limit of a single call and was cut off, so it ran again as a background shell.

What I left alone:
- **.run/P41/T8/ghidra.bak (1 GB):** the developer is keeping it (R70); deleting it is the open decision from T8's review.
- **.run/ghidra_rebuild/:** this is the rebuild tool's own state folder, and ghidra_roster.py reads its .proof files. It sits outside .run/<phase>/ by the tool's design.
- **.run/plans/:** both drafts are superseded by the approved plans.

Recommendation: the rest of .run/P41 is about 3.4 GB in total (t2 1.2 GB, T4 1.0 GB, T8 1.0 GB including ghidra.bak). Everything except ghidra.bak can be deleted after the close.

The steps c2 and c3 could affect were re-run and are OK: the types check, the roster check and `make kit-corpus`. **What was built** - **Generator:** a new `tools/ghidra_types.py` writes 7,151 type rows (4,131 structs, 3,005 typedefs, 15 unions; the headers have no enums) into `config/ghidra/types/<prog>.jsonl`. Each program gets the types its translation units use, closed over the types they depend on. - The two prototypes have no translation units, so they take main's set. - The 6,014 types no program uses go to SLUS_007.26, so every type is in at least one program. - **Where the rows live:** generated rows stay out of the hand-authored `config/ghidra/<prog>.jsonl` files. The rebuild merges both into export order, and the proof compares the delta against that merged file. - **Staleness check:** `--check` takes about 6 s and is wired into tools-health. It fails on a stale or missing file, a type placed in no program, or a header member the parser dropped. - **Ghidra scripts:** the import and export scripts gained bitfield support, and the import gained dependency ordering for typedefs and unions and a fix for two-dimensional arrays. - **Function-pointer arrays:** three types made of arrays of function pointers came out as 1-byte shells. The generator now reads those members with `cdecl.py`, so the census's layout engine is unchanged. - **Wiki:** the Ghidra rebuild page has a section on how types reach the database. **Rows and proof wall times** | Program | Rows | Proof wall time | |---|---|---| | SLUS_007.26 | 6,565 | 208 s | | sep8_SLUS_007.26 | 394 | 206 s | | aug31_USA_DEMO.EXE | 394 | 208 s | | ov_SC01_077 | 593 | 172 s | | ov_SC06_018 | 600 | 173 s | | resident | 59 | 77 s | **Controls** - **Mutation:** changing one field of one type in a scratch copy of resident's rows (u32 to u16) gave PROOF FAIL. Restoring it gave PASS. - **Layout against gcc:** the offsets and sizes in Ghidra's own export of SLUS_007.26 equal gcc's for all 3,772 types (5 unions and 7 bitfield structs included). The generated rows of all 6 files also equal gcc's: 5,221 types, 34,310 members. - The proof only shows that Ghidra reproduces the rows exactly; the gcc comparison is what checks the layout. **For Claude and T8:** the MCP is still stopped. Phase 3.41 is finished and needs your word to close it. Its milestone gate passed in full at commit f4b1fbad3d, from a clean tree. All 218 game binaries still rebuild byte-identical, and all six Ghidra proofs pass.

**What the phase did.** It made the struct types' checks able to pass honestly, and put the types into Ghidra.
- **The type gate works now.** `type_census --check` used to fail on every one of about 176,000 raw casts, so it could never pass. It now checks what "done" means: no duplicate structs, no dead names, structs defined only in the shared headers, and no unrecorded lying declarations. Raw casts are still counted, and the separate cast-ledger check still requires a reason for each one. The type check now runs inside `make tools-health`, which takes about 17 minutes.
- **Return-type lies.** These are declarations whose return type disagrees with the function's real definition. They went from 273 functions on 100,266 declaration lines to 19 functions on 9,302 lines. Each of those 19 is a width or sign mismatch, where fixing either side changes the compiled code. Each one is recorded in `config/lying_exceptions.tsv` along with the instruction that moves.
- **Hints must name their instrument.** Every marked compiler hint, the `// !FAKE:` markers, must now name the tool or batch that judged it, and `lever_census` fails otherwise. One bare marker was found and fixed.
- **Types in Ghidra.** A new tool, `tools/ghidra_types.py`, turns the two headers into Ghidra types: 7,151 of them, including structs, typedefs and unions. Every type's fields match gcc's layout, and a check fails whenever the headers change and the Ghidra rows weren't regenerated. Your live Ghidra database now holds them, and you confirmed the struct T8 showed you.
- **Three small tool bugs from 3.40 are fixed:** the `--no-cache` crash, `struct_twins` reading a stale census, and `lift_types --fold` leaving an old comment behind.
- **The record.**
  - **Graph:** the readability series and the README graph now show return-type lies.
  - **Day-1 guide:** it has the lesson: declare each function once, in one shared header.
  - **Story and retrospective:** story §10, retrospective §7 and the wiki run to Phase 41, and a new wiki page, "The type layer", explains the gates.
  - **Cookbook and backups:** eight cookbook entries were banked, and the ledgers are backed up.

**Left for you after the close:**
- **Push the archive clone** at `/home/musashi/BFM-decomp-archive`, which holds the new commit b9534ed. Then push this repo as usual.
- **Bring Ghidra back:** the MCP Ghidra is stopped for the proofs. When you want it, start it with `bash tools/ghidra_mcp_start.sh`, then run `/mcp`.
- **The 1 GB backup:** `.run/P41/T8/ghidra.bak` is still kept. T12 suggests deleting it, along with the other roughly 2.4 GB of scratch in `.run/P41/`, which nothing needs. Tell me if you want any of that done.
- **The PA3 bug report:** file the wait-for issue with `/pa-bugreport`, using the drafted text. That's still open.

**Parked for later phases:**
- **3.43:**
  - the 37 functions that became `s32` with no `return` statement;
  - the 9 doubtful wall tags.
- **3.45:** the license.

The close question is open in the picker.

---

Yes, 3.42 matters. It is the groundwork for the generation's main payoff, which is real names in the code, and 3.43 can't start until it's done. It renames nothing itself. It builds the machinery that lets the next phase name things safely and in the right order.

**What it builds.** These three pieces come from its entry in `/home/musashi/bfm-decomp/GENERATION_PLAN.md`, line 18:

- **A rename scanner.** Once the project names something, say turning `func_8016130C` into a real name, every reference to it has to change. That covers tens of thousands of source files, the symbol files and the Ghidra mirror. The scanner checks that nothing was missed. It must prove itself twice: once on one real rename, and once on a deliberately planted miss that it has to catch. Without it, a missed reference only shows up when something breaks later.
- **The shared-function form.** The game copies the same function into many overlays at different addresses. The last count, from Phase 3.35, was 3,801 such functions with 30,347 copies on disk. Today each copy is its own source file. 3.42 turns each family into one shared source, written once and used at every address, using the existing `SHARED_FN` form (so far only 6 places use it). The same goes for the 20 trampoline families, small stubs that just jump to another function. The payoff is that naming or fixing a function once covers every copy. Otherwise 3.43 would name the same function hundreds of times.
- **A leverage ranking.** This is a list of which data and functions are worth naming first: how often each one is referenced, which structs it owns, and how many copies a shared body has. It is written down with the total it's ranked against. 3.43's milestone is "name the top 500 of this ranking", so 3.42 decides where all the naming effort goes.

**What it must not change.** The game's bytes. All 218 binaries must still rebuild byte-identical, and the Ghidra proofs must still pass. The work only changes how the source is organised.

**How big it is.** The shared-function move is the heavy part, since it touches thousands of files. The 3,801 and 30,347 counts are seven phases old, so the plan's first job is to count them again. It also takes on the P3.41-1 item: run the cast check against the census the graph series uses before the phase's closing graph snapshot, then regenerate the timeline.

That is why max effort for its planning seems reasonable to me: 3.43 and 3.44 build on this plan.

The picker about setting the planner to max is still open. Once you answer, I'll start the fresh planner.

- **The journal's month index is complete.** It now lists every agent's entries. It used to leave out about a third.
- **Smaller changes.** Updates are now named by what they bring, and the docs use the new plan names: Ultra, Max and Pro.

PA3 is updated from 3.32.11 to 3.32.12. It's a small hotfix with two fixes:

- **Updates now say when they've loaded.** After an update installed itself at session start, PA3 could lose track of it. Then no restart confirmed the update was loaded, and I kept asking you for one more restart. Now the first message after the restart always says "installed and loaded", and work goes on. This release is the last time that can happen, which may explain why the 3.32.11 restart didn't seem to take.
- **Restarted experts keep their context across days.** When a task's expert is cut off without saving its progress note, PA3 writes one for the fresh expert from the journal. It used to miss everything from earlier days, because finished days are packed into one file. It now reads those too, so a task that runs past midnight doesn't lose its notes.

Project Architect 3.32.12, a hotfix (Updates that say they loaded, and notes kept across midnight), is installed.

**To load it: /exit, then claude --continue (not /clear)**

The mod's own files changed, so nothing starts until you restart. After that, I'll start the 3.42 planner at max.

I've started the fresh planner for Phase 3.42 at max effort, which is now its built-in setting, and it's drafting the plan to `/home/musashi/bfm-decomp/.run/plans/phase-3.42.md`. There was no session hold left to release, so I started it directly.

**What 3.42 is for.** It lays the groundwork for naming. 3.43 is the phase that actually names things, and it can't start without this. 3.42 renames nothing itself.

**What it builds:**
- **A rename scanner.** It proves a rename rewrote every reference to the symbol. It is tested once on a real rename and once on a miss we plant on purpose.
- **The shared-function form.** About 3,801 functions are copied at more than one address. Each gets one shared source, so naming one names every copy. The 20 trampoline families are shared the same way.
- **A ranking.** It ranks data and functions by how much naming each one pays off. 3.43 then names the top 500 from it.

**What must not change:** the game's bytes. All 218 binaries must still rebuild byte-identical, and the Ghidra proofs must still pass.

**How big it is.** The shared-function move is the large part, since it touches thousands of files. The counts are from Phase 3.35, so the plan's first job is to measure them again.

The brief also covers these:
- **Last phase's leftover check.** The planner should fold it into the milestone-gate task: run the cast check against the graph's census before the closing snapshot, then regenerate the timeline.
- **The earlier planner's research.** One report from the stopped planner did land, and this planner reuses it instead of redoing the work.
- **The MCP Ghidra.** It's stopped, so the plan will mark any task that needs the live server.

When the draft is ready, I'll open it in the plan view and tell you in a line or two what needs your decision.

## Rationale
- Order: baselines first (T1, R83). The harness before the campaign (docs/gen3-handoff.md:233-235): names ledger and scanner (T2), then the rename tool with its two controls (T3). The shared form: writer, ledger re-keying and a pilot (T4) before the fleet; C classes (T5) before D and B (T6), because one name-blind text per class is nearly byte-neutral by construction and proves the batch loop at volume, while a D class needs an exemplar and a B class a registry repair. Trampolines (T7) once the parameter machinery exists. The ranking (T8) counts per unique body, so it does not depend on the conversion's progress and may run while a conversion task waits. Record (T9), gate (T10), close docs (T11), cleanup (T12).
- Known rename in scratch: the scope puts the machinery before any name, and 3.43 owns names; a worktree rename (C0546) proves the tool, the scanner and the per-binary gates without landing one. The control is independent of the scanner: its candidate set is compared with a plain `git grep -n -i` of every spelling.
- Planted miss of a kind the byte gate cannot see (a ledger row, a registry member, a tool control, a comment): a stale call already fails the link, so the scanner proves its value where the build is blind; T3 shows the gates green on it and the scanner red.
- Scanner and rename tool are separate programs and the scanner does not import the tool's matcher: one shared matcher would hide its own blind spot.
- Ledger fields are rule 1's (docs/gen3-standards.md:49-59). A `guess_` name without a basis row fails from 3.42 on (0 today, so free); curated names without a citation row are counted, not gated (3.43 gates them).
- Ghidra once: the only steps that need :8080 free are T3's mirror leg and the six proofs; both run in T10, so the developer's server keeps running until the gate.
- Effort: T8 is the one `effort: high` task: how xrefs, struct ownership and fan-out combine into one rank is a judgment no test arbitrates (docs/decision-log.md:1655-1667 warns that nins x reach over-counts); its coverage, denominators and two independent counts are tested.
- Coder tiers: all in-house (card). The stop rule for classes the gate rejects is the developer's (Developer decides).

## Cookbook
- C0502 (§453) one source per unique function: the include-at-site share, its gate and four defects (a checkout wipes uncommitted shares; a twin's instance resolves to its primary's TU; never extend the registry before the gate; add_members_surgical skips the verbose `members:` form) — T4-T7.
- C0134 (§123) dedup_propagate is h_exact-only; a tool's refusal is a statement about the tool — T4-T6.
- C0017 (§11) cross-binary dedup and code sharing — T4.
- C0187 (§176b) batch-gating mechanics when many edits land in one .c — T5, T6.
- C0468 (§420) a multi-cluster symbol rebase, and the bare-name dedup that hid three quarters of it — T2, T3.
- C0008 (§7) PsyQ SDK types and symbols (the rename procedure at :8-9, R3.42-002) — T3.
- C0538 a split rename leaves stale oracle baseline objects and recipes.json — T3.
- C0546 scratch git worktree for reproducing an in-place tool's edits — T3, T10.
- C0536 `verbatim_check --update` keeps ov_* fleet rows; drop a decompiled one by hand — T7.
- C0513 Makefile health-gate traps — T2, T6, T10.
- C0514 run a repo-wide check on unedited HEAD first; mirrored edits need `make kit-corpus` — T1, T9.
- C0550 new tool files need `git add -N` before `make kit-corpus` — T2, T3, T8.
- C0553 kit_lint rejects project tokens in tool_dictionary prose — T9.
- C0554 Ghidra live-DB import and MCP over SSE — T10.
- C0545 strip a JSONL ledger for public — T10 (ledger backup).
- Grep `cookbook/INDEX.md` for `share`, `dedup`, `symbol`, `rename`, `residue`, `verbatim`, `ghidra` before each task.

## Developer decides
- The Ghidra server is running, not stopped. A headless Ghidra started at 16:33 today serves the live database on port 8080, although Claude Code did not connect to it this session. Every Ghidra step in this phase (the six proofs and the rename mirror test) refuses while it runs, and no task needs the live server. Recommended: approve now one clean stop with tools/ghidra_mcp_stop.sh when T10 starts (it saves, then stops, never kills; R49). It stays stopped until the close; then tools/ghidra_mcp_start.sh and your /mcp bring it back. Until T10 it keeps running for your own use.
- Stop rule for classes the byte gate rejects in the shared form. The milestone says 0 left. A shared text can fail to compile byte-identically in some file (a declaration in scope, a jump table, an -O0 split file); each such class is recorded with its measured reason. Recommended: the strictest rule, 0 left: if any class is still rejected after the repair steps, T5 or T6 stops after sorting them by reason, and Claude brings you the groups before the conversion counts as done (the same rule 3.41 used for the return-type lies, R43).

Scanner per R32: for each renamed symbol the candidate set over-approximates (the old name, `func_` or `D_` with 6-8 hex digits in either case, `0x<ADDR>` in either case, the bare address) over every git-tracked text file; every hit is classified rewritten, history (a short closed path list: cookbook/, phase-ends/, docs/decision-log.md, docs/story.md, docs/retrospective.md, docs/retired/, docs/research-archive/, .claude-state/) or stale; candidates must equal classified, so an unclassified hit fails. It reads a rename spec or a git range. lint_symbol_refs.py stays or is folded in; the two may never disagree. ## Risks
- Ledger re-keying is the conversion's main failure mode (R3.42-004): every gate keys on the file path, so a batch converted without re-keying turns lever_census `--strict --residue`, type_census `--check-casts --residue`, the lying check and verbatim_check red at many rows. T4 proves the re-keying on the pilot before the fleet.
- Context-dependent texts: a shared text compiled in another file can differ (declarations in scope, a `__FILE__` use, a jump-table carve, an -O0 split file); the gate rejects it and the reason codes exist (JTBL-CARVE, O0-LOCAL, TU-CONFLICT, GATE-REJECT, CROSS-SPACE). If many classes end there, "0 left" is not reachable without the developer's stop rule.
- The text oracle: at `--scope all` the deferred same-address duplicate texts (377 texts, 1,636 sites) count as violations; T1 checks that each belongs to a cross-address class, or the milestone's S1 0 needs more work than the 3,801.
- D exemplars may drop a legibility gain present in another copy or carry a lever marker to every site; the rule is logged per class, and `--reexemplar` exists.
- Metric drops from de-duplication: lever and raw-cast site counts fall as copies collapse into headers; the series and the docs must say why (T9, T11).
- Scanner false comfort: the candidate set must include every spelling (C0468: a bare-name dedup hid three quarters of a rebase); the positive control compares it with an independent grep. An over-broad history list hides stale references; it is a short closed list, reviewed in T2's summary.
- Name-ordered linking: a data rename can move bytes where the linker orders symbols by name (common symbols, sorted sections); the per-binary gate is the arbiter, and any such case becomes a cookbook entry.
- Trampoline callee inside an `__asm__` string: stringification or an operand may not survive maspsx; T7 proves one family first.
- Ghidra: the running server blocks every headless step, and the stop needs the developer's OK (R49); the rebuild and R22 must never overlap (`make clean` wipes build/ghidra_rebuild/proj).
- tools-health is longer than one 285 s tool call; the card's "foreground" note predates 3.41 T5's addition; 3.41 T10 and T12 ran it through tools/run.sh.
- Evidence kinds for 3.43: rule 1's list has no kind for a name proven by matching Sony's library objects (69 of the 1,080 curated names carry such a note); 3.43's citation rows will need the developer's amendment, and 3.42 keeps the list in one constant.
- Field and type renames are compiler-checked in build inputs; the scanner covers linker symbols, and 3.43's planner decides whether fields need its non-build scan.
- Disk: 26 GB free; the worktree build and R22 fit; .run/ holds 19 GB, including .run/P41 (R87).

## 174237-016r-claude-answer
---
ts: 2026-11-09T00:42:37.520Z
session: 252d32b5-eb3b-4ca8-9931-8e47bb99cd41
agentId: ac896f50928dd4e7a
model: claude-opus-5-5

turnId: 63b9ce3a-3bd1-40e7-a75c-b8945f29e5ed
usage: {input: 10, cacheRead: 378405, cacheWrite: 3870, output: 1328}
---

Nothing went wrong in 3.41. Its corpus record can only be written after the phase is archived, so the next phase's first task always adds it. T1 did exactly that.

**What a corpus record is.** The repo includes a kit, `decomp-architect/`. It's the day-one starter pack this project hands to someone starting a new decomp: the order of work, the rules, the tools. Its `corpus/` folder holds exact copies of the project's tools and of each phase's archived record, the PhaseEnd file, so the kit carries real evidence of how things went. A check inside `make tools-health` compares each copy with its original and reports any copy that's missing or out of date. `make kit-corpus` refreshes the copies.

**Why 3.41's copy wasn't there yet.** The close creates `PhaseEnd_Phase3.41.md` as its very last step, after every task has finished. So no task in 3.41 could have copied it. The same thing happened last time: 3.40's record was added by 3.41's T1, in commit 9adb323306. The other gap T1 found has the same cause. The 3.32.11 and 3.32.12 updates changed `tools/plan_edit.py`, so its kit copy was stale.

**Was it supposed to be done?** Yes, and it was, at the earliest point it could be. Each phase's T1 runs `tools-health`, sees the gap, refreshes the copies and commits them. Here that was commit bcd6a3f2a3, before T1 took its baselines.

**My recommendation: leave it as is.** Moving the copy into the close isn't possible, because PA3 runs the archive and the project can't add a step after it. And the one-commit fix at the next phase start is cheap and always caught.

T1 carries on: the rebuild passed 218/218 at the new commit, and `tools-health` runs next. I've released the talk hold.

topic: Kit corpus records
idea: "why didn't 3.41 phase have a corpus record yet? was it supposed to? whats a corpus record?"
refinement: decomp-architect/corpus/ holds exact copies of the project's tools and of each archived PhaseEnd. tool_census --check, inside make tools-health, reports a missing or stale copy, and make kit-corpus refreshes them. The close writes PhaseEnd_Phase<n>.md as its last step, after every task, so no task in that phase can copy it. The next phase's T1 always catches the gap: 3.40's record was added in 9adb323306, and 3.41's in bcd6a3f2a3. PA3 updates that change tools/plan_edit.py leave its kit copy stale the same way.
decision: Leave it as is. Each phase's T1 refreshes the kit corpus (the closed phase's PhaseEnd and any tool copies a PA3 update changed) before taking baselines. No step is added to the close, which PA3 runs and the project cannot extend.
  binds work: no
  landed: discussion only
headline: A missing corpus record for the just-closed phase is expected; the next phase's T1 refreshes it

topic: Phase 3.42 T1 pause for a Claude Code restart
idea: "tell the expert to paus work after his r22 finishes, so i can restart claude"
refinement: Claude steered T1 with pause: true: finish the R22 in hand, commit what is green, write the progress note, start nothing new. T1's expert handed back and is held, not restarted. Before the pause, T1 had refreshed the kit corpus (bcd6a3f2a3), and R22 had passed 218/218 at that HEAD.
open: T1 is paused for the developer's Claude Code restart -> on his word, Claude calls release({hold: "stopped"})
headline: T1 paused with its progress saved, for the developer's restart; it resumes on his word

PA3 is updated from 3.32.12 to 3.32.14. This covers two small hotfixes, and both are display fixes.

- **3.32.13: interrupted agents are charged to your account.** After you interrupted Claude or an expert, the Session line could show ?/? for a while. An agent still running when its session ended was recorded with no account attached. It's now charged to the account the session is on. A one-time repair also reassigns turns that were already recorded without an account, so your weekly figures include them.
- **3.32.14: failed shells clear from the statusline too.** 3.32.11 made a failed background shell disappear from the band after 2 minutes. Now it also leaves the statusline after 2 minutes, instead of staying red until its agent reports.

Project Architect 3.32.14, a hotfix (Red shell lines leave the statusline too), is installed.

The ledger will be config/names.tsv with one row per name and a set of columns covering kind, scope, address, tier, basis, confidence, evidence, conflict, history, and citation. Right now there are no guess_ names in it. I'm weighing whether the 2,691 curated names should each get a row, and whether the count of curated names missing a citation row should be printed.

I'm weighing whether the ledger should be seeded with rows for the 2,691 curated names or start empty and only gain rows as names land, with curated names lacking a citation row counted and printed. Starting empty fits better, since seeding that many rows with no evidence would be noise. I also need to check for dead rows, meaning names that appear in no build input, and for any `guess_` token in src/ that has no basis.

I'm planning the scanner to take either a rename spec file or a git diff range, and for each rename it will search tracked text files for the old name, the func_ or D_ forms with the address, and the bare address in either case. Each hit then needs to be sorted into rewritten, history, or stale, with stale split by whether the build can see it.

I'm working out how to classify each hit of a rename. Hits on the exact old name go to stale unless they sit in a history path, while address forms count as rewritten when the new name is on the same line, history when they're in a history file, and otherwise unclassified and failing. The open question is how a legitimate address mention, like a memory-map doc listing the address beside the new name, gets exempted without loosening the check.

Still settling the unclassified case: an address-form hit that isn't on a history path, doesn't share a line with the new name, and isn't the old name itself counts as a failure rather than getting a special allow mechanism. The renamer resolves it by mentioning the new name on that line. I'm also checking that the candidate set and the classified set come out equal.

I'm settling the matching rules now: placeholder names like func_ or D_ followed by the address match case-insensitively on the hex digits, and I'm weighing whether the bare address should also match with leading zeros stripped, since some addresses are shorter than eight digits.

I'm settling how the scanner finds and sorts the hits: it'll search every git-tracked text file for the old name, the placeholder forms with the address, and the bare hex address, matching case-insensitively. Renames can come from a spec file or from a diff of the symbol files across a git range. Since git grep is fast, I'll use it with one combined pattern, and I'll treat anything under src/, include/, or the symbol configs as build input.

I'm settling the lint question: the scanner's stale-build-input check for a func_ address in src/ should match exactly what lint_symbol_refs flags, so the two can't disagree.

I'm now checking whether lint_symbol_refs can be reused: the scanner should share its symbol-loading and token regex rather than duplicate a parser, and a selftest fixture with a stale `func_` reference in src/ should get the same result from both. If lint has no importable function, I'll pull its logic into one with a small refactor. I'm also planning ledger checks that validate kind, tier, confidence, and evidence against closed lists.

I'm defining the ledger checks: each row's kind, tier, confidence, and evidence must come from closed lists, and a guess_ name needs a basis, evidence, and confidence, while a cited tier needs a citation. I'll also flag duplicate name-and-scope rows and dead rows whose name doesn't appear in its source, then add a `--gate-citations` option that fails when curated names lack a citation.

I'm settling how the selftest will check the classifier: rather than building a temporary git repo, which would sit awkwardly under the phase rules, I'll keep the classification core pure, so it takes hit lists and ledger rows as plain data and the selftest can feed it synthetic cases directly.

  - The dictionary row's phase column is `P10`, not `P3.42`. That column holds the kit's phase group (P1..P10), which is also the corpus folder (`corpus/tools/P10/`). The 3.41 tools ghidra_types and type_census are filed as P10 too.
  
  **For you**
  - Tracked `.run/P32/*` logs and the `decomp-architect/corpus/` copies are not under HISTORY. So any real rename will report them as stale-invisible or unclassified hits. Whether to add them to HISTORY is your call.
  - `--range` can't see the first naming of a splat auto-name, because that is an add with no remove; use `--spec` for those.
  - `git add -N` leaves an index entry. `git add -N` is what C0550 prescribes.
  - The guard blocks `sed`/`grep` on `config/tool_dictionary.tsv` (over 20k chars), so I edited it with a small script.
  - I did not stage files I didn't change: `docs/story-timeline.md`, `docs/struct-map.md`, `.claude-state/journal/*`, `phase-ends/DISCUSSION_INDEX.md`, `.run/P35`, `.run/P36`.

done

T2 is finished. The project now has a names ledger, `config/names.tsv`, and a rename scanner, `tools/rename_scan.py`. `make tools-health` is OK with both checks in it.

**Names ledger.** It has rule 1's 11 fields: name, kind, scope, address, tier, basis, confidence, evidence kind, conflict flag, history and citation. The kind, tier, confidence and evidence-kind lists (rule 1's six) are each one constant in the tool. It is header-only for now: the tree has 0 `guess_` names, and adding rows for the existing curated names without citations would mean inventing evidence.

**Rename scanner.**
- **Coverage check.** It searches every tracked text file for every spelling of the old name: the name itself, func_/D_ plus the address, 0x plus the address, and the bare address, in either case. Each hit is classified as history, rewritten, stale in a build input, stale outside the build, or unclassified. It asserts that the count of candidate hits equals the count classified, and that every hit belongs to some rename (R32). Any stale or unclassified hit fails.
- **Input.** It reads a rename spec (`--spec`) or a git range (`--range`).
- **Ledger check.** `--ledger` on the tree reports 0 `guess_` names without a basis row and 0 dead rows. It prints 1,083 curated names with no citation row; that number is not gated, and `--gate-citations` turns it into a gate for 3.43.
- **Self-test.** `--selftest` passes on all seven required fixtures.
- **lint_symbol_refs.py.** It stays as its own gate. It was refactored just enough that both tools share one symbol-file parser, and its output is byte-identical to before. A self-test case checks that both flag the same stale token in `src/`.

**Test rename.** In a scratch worktree, renaming func_80037004 to a `guess_` name gave:
- 25 history hits
- 1 rewritten
- 5 stale in build inputs (`src/800_b_2.c:123`, `src/800_c.c:1600`, ...)
- 51 stale outside the build
- 5 unclassified (e.g. a bare address in the corpus copy of `share_census.py:604`)

It exited 1, as it should. A real rename reaches well beyond `src/`.

**My two edits after the coder (in the plan's T2 commit):**
- `--range` now treats a symbol line added where no line was removed as a first naming from func_/D_<ADDR>. The coder's version found no rename there, and that is 3.43's main case.
- `.run/` joins the history paths, because its tracked files are raw logs and generated output that are never hand-edited.

**Other notes:**
- The 1,083 is unique names across the 219 symbol files the binaries use, proto files excluded. T1's 2,691 counted every occurrence, proto files included.
- The new tool's `config/tool_dictionary.tsv` row has phase P10, the kit's phase group, as for 3.41's tools.
- A rename's hits in the tool copies under `decomp-architect/corpus/` stay stale until `make kit-corpus` reruns. The recipe is banked as cookbook C0555.

The project now has a rename tool, and both controls passed in a throwaway worktree. No rename landed in the main tree: `git grep guess_ClearTbl40` over src, the symbol files, the registry and names.tsv finds nothing.

`tools/rename_sym.py --spec` performs a rename end to end:
- It writes the line into the right `config/symbols.<alias>.txt`.
- For a share class (`class:<id>`), it writes one line per member, with distinct names (new, new_2) when one binary holds two members.
- It rewrites every live mention, including ledgers and tool controls, and leaves history alone.
- It never edits generated files; it prints the command that regenerates each one.
- It adds the names.tsv row and prints the touched binaries and Ghidra programs.

**Positive control** (func_80037004 renamed to guess_ClearTbl40, fixture `config/rename_known.tsv`):
- `make extract BINARY=main` ran, and main is the only touched binary (Ghidra program SLUS_007.26).
- `gate_main --assert-baseline` was GREEN at the 143dbb89 baseline.
- The scanner exited 0: 89 candidates all classified, 0 stale, 0 unclassified.
- A separate `git grep -n -i` search over every old spelling found the same 89 lines; the diff is empty.
- `rename_scan.py --ledger` and `lint_symbol_refs` both passed.

**Negative control**: I left `config/dedup.us.yaml:60` unrewritten. The gate stayed green, and the scanner exited 1 naming only that line.

`rename_scan.py --selftest` passes, which I re-ran. tools-health passed at the coder's final commit.

Two rules are new, and I've recorded both as binding decisions:
- **Share headers keep their names.** `func_<VRAM>.h` files are named by address, so a rename never renames them; the scanner puts those tokens in a new `address-key` class. Renaming headers would mean moving files and updating the registry and every ledger keyed by file path.
- **Two closed lists cover mentions a rename can't make stale.**
  - Address-only mentions pass only in the files on `ADDRESS_KEYED`, such as the address-keyed checks in `tools/share_census.py`.
  - `func_<vram>` names written by a generator pass only on `NAMES_FROM_VRAM`, which holds just `docs/duplicates.md`. Its generator names every main function `func_<vram>`, so without this the positive control could never pass. The coder added this list and I accepted it.

**Deviations:**
- The rename was committed inside the worktree, because `gate_main` checks out main's files and would have reverted it.
- My brief told the coder to probe with `make -n extract`. In this Makefile that actually runs the extract, and it turned one tools-health run red; the rerun was green. This trap is already in the cookbook as C0513.
- names.tsv uses scope `us` for main, because the ledger check takes binary names from the symbol-file names.

Commits: 7ceaea748f, 4a5ca7492f, 1fef4c41d5, 1026ef1749 (coder) and d516f891a3 (my log and summary). I banked the worktree rename procedure as cookbook entry C0556. - It writes the line into the right `config/symbols.<alias>.txt`.

  **What changed in /home/musashi/bfm-decomp/tools/share_body.py**
  - A new function, `rekey_restruct`, runs inside the cross bucket's re-key, after `rekey_ledgers` and before the residue steps. For each header it appends one rung-S row keyed (header, SHARED_FN), built from its private copies' latest rung-S rows, `KEPT(<cls>)` entries included.
    - Entry lines are shifted by (header def line − private def line).
    - Copies are merged as a multiset max-union, and any difference is logged.
    - The row takes its verdict from a copy whose verdict is in `BACKED_S_VERDICTS`.
    - Old rows are never rewritten.
    - A header whose latest row already has `rekey_of` is skipped, which makes it idempotent.
  - Each move now also records the private def line.
  - The repair entry point is `--rekey-batch <label>`. It rebuilds the moves from `batch_<label>.json` plus each header's `#define SHARED_FN` / include sites. It gets each private def line from the pre-batch text at the commit named in the header's first comment, then runs the full re-key and residue steps.

  - 92 header rows were copied; 10 of them needed a union because their copies' rows really differ. 179 private copies had no rung-S row.
  - The other ledgers re-keyed 0 rows (already done in T4.c2). The residue step added 0 rows, restamp 0 rows, and check-residue found 0 uncovered.

  - The merge ignores the `line` field except for `KEPT(REINTERP)` entries, which are the only ones the gate matches by line.
  - Reason: the old rows' lines drift from copy to copy (def-relative offsets differ by up to about 80). Merging by line multiplied the entries: the first dry run flagged 73 headers, and one went from 67 entries to 120.

---

done

Functions whose identical bytes sit at more than one address can now get one source, and a 122-class pilot is converted with every gate green.

**What the tool does**
- `tools/share_body.py --bucket cross` writes one header per class. Same-space classes go to `src/shared/<space>/cls_<h12>.h`, cross-space ones to `src/shared/multi/`.
- The header's first comment is the provenance line: class hash, registry id `X_<h12>`, generator and commit.
- Each private copy becomes the `#define SHARED_FN <name>` / `#include` / `#undef SHARED_FN` triple at its position, and the class is registered with `func: SHARED_FN` and members {binary, vram, name}.
- Names are keyed by the class hash, not a function name, so a 3.43 rename only touches the `#define` lines and the registry's member names.
- In the same batch the tool re-keys the ledgers onto the header: lever residue, lying exceptions and verbatim rows. It also copies the restruct rung-S rows, including the `KEPT` entries, to the header and runs restruct `--residue`, `--restamp` and `--check-residue` on the touched segments with `--only`.
- `--rekey-batch <label>` repeats the re-key for a batch already committed.
- `share_body_cycle.sh` takes the bucket as a 4th argument.
- A class the gate rejects is undone at every site and listed in `batch_<label>.json`, not ledgered. That keeps the violation drop exact and leaves R92's bucketing to T5/T6.

**Pilot: 122 classes**
- Two probe classes, then batch `cross1` of 120. The mix: 2-address classes, 3-or-more-address classes, 5 cross-space classes (in `src/shared/multi/`), and twin pairs including ov_SC01_005/006.
- The batch gated 161/161 binaries green with 0 rejected.

**Gates**
- R22: 218/218.
- `make tools-health`: OK at f6447a87e3.
- The verify command (`share_census --check --scope all --strict-macros --strict-text`): S1 violations 3,789 at T1, 3,667 now, a drop of exactly 122.
- Census controls read as at T1 except twin symmetry, which went from 631/631 to 627/627 and is still equal. Four pilot classes changed it: 42ed21e35548, a3b394ef369a, eec35d73e20c, 37495cbe70b2.

**Fixes beyond the plan's file list**
The first full tools-health run showed that a moved body is also tracked in places the plan didn't list. Three fixes followed:
- `tools/argcheck.py` now binds each site's own name to the header. Without it, 75 lying sites came up unledgered.
- The residue step runs before restamp and covers forms P, I, X and M.
- The rung-S rows, `KEPT` entries included, are copied to the header. Without that, 37 casts came up unbacked.

**Not yet exercised**
- No pilot site had a lever, lying or verbatim row, so 0 of those rows were re-keyed. The lying-row re-key and the line shift for `KEPT(REINTERP)` entries are untested.
- `cross1` ran through `share_body.py` directly, not through `share_body_cycle.sh`. The cycle still commits with a plain `git commit`, which commit_task.sh forbids, so it is unexercised for `cross`.

**Recommendation for T5/T6:** run cross batches through `share_body.py` and commit_task.sh, or move the cycle's commit onto commit_task.sh first. Check the line shift on the first batch that has a `KEPT(REINTERP)` entry.

**Commits:** d3f940420a, c8d0f1dc89, e780be897a, f6447a87e3, d05ea62517, 3d86b5effe. The technique and its traps are banked as cookbook C0557.
